> DEVELOPER

Simplify it for me. What's the deal with the calibration? Minimize cognitive load, please.

> AGENT

The fitter reports each burst's brightness in **whatever raw units its `.npy` file happens to be in** — and CHIME and DSA were never put on the same scale (no flux calibration). So a CHIME number and a DSA number aren't in the same currency. Three consequences: 1. You can't compare CHIME's `c0` to DSA's `c0` — different unknown units. 2. Adding them (`E_CHIME + E_DSA`) is adding dollars to yen. Worse: the mix differs per burst, so even *ranking* bursts by total energy is unreliable. 3. The DSA spectral index slamming to −5 is probably just the uncorrected DSA bandpass shape leaking in, not real physics. **So:** the energy table isn't trustworthy as-is. Fix = pick one band, tie it to a published fluence, report that alone as a rough number. Don't sum the two.

> DEVELOPER

Okay, so it sounds like in order to actually get to any sort of quoted energy or quotable energy, we need to look more into how we can actually extract units that agree from these data. That seems like it would require digging into the actual telescope backends. Is that true? Or is the approach different from that?

> AGENT

Not the backends. You need each band independently put on the **absolute flux scale (Jy)** — and once both are in Jy, they agree automatically. "Make the two agree with each other" is the wrong frame; you map each to physical units separately, and Jy is Jy. Two ways to get there, cheapest first: 1. **Borrow published fluences.** CHIME/FRB catalog already quotes per-burst fluence in Jy·ms (their beam/system calibration done for you). DSA-110's pipeline likewise produces calibrated fluences. Scale your fitted spectral *shape* to the published fluence per band → arbitrary `c0` becomes Jy·ms. No reprocessing. 2. **Standard flux calibration** (if no published number): radiometer equation with each band's SEFD / system temperature, anchored to a known calibrator or noise-diode injection. That's **pipeline-level**, not backend electronics. So the real question isn't backends — it's **provenance of the `.npy`**: is each waterfall already calibrated (Jy) or raw S/N? If DSA's reduction already applied SEFD/cal, you may just be missing the metadata, and the fix is bookkeeping, not new analysis. Then skip the catalog — use the **radiometer equation**, the standard single-burst route: ``` fluence [Jy·ms] ≈ (S/N) × SEFD / √(n_pol · Δν · Δt_samp) (integrated over the pulse) ``` You already have most of it: the fitter whitens by off-pulse MAD, so the data is **effectively in S/N units already**. The genuinely hard part is the **beam/position correction**: sensitivity depends where in each primary beam the burst landed. CHIME's transit beam is the dominant uncertainty (factor-of-few, asymmetric) — which is exactly why FRB fluences always carry fat error bars. DSA needs its beamformed-gain/position too. So realistically: - **Need:** per-band SEFD + beam response at each burst's sky position. That's a number from the CHIME and DSA co-detection teams, not a code dig. - **Get:** order-of-magnitude fluences with honest large error bars — appropriate for FRB energetics anyway. The geometry for a transit array: - Primary-beam boresight sits at (RA = LST, Dec = pointing_dec); a burst is detected at transit, so HA ≈ 0 → **θ ≈ |Dec_src − Dec_pointing|**, φ along the meridian. With same-RA source and boresight, the astropy separation *is* exactly the Dec difference. - Your examples cluster at Dec 70–74°, so if the survey pointing Dec is ~71–73° the offsets are ~1–3° — right where the beam falls to ~half power (dsa_beam FWHM≈3.6°). So G genuinely varies and is worth computing, not assuming G=1. One caveat: the HA offset is only ≈0 if the burst arrived near the source's meridian transit; the primary beam spans ±~6 min in HA, so there's a small E-W term. Dec dominates; I'll treat HA as a documented small correction (addable from burst UTC vs transit time). Updating Phase 3 to make this the primary path:

This also undoes the sensitivity weighting that rails γ_D. - CHIME singlebeam = pre-flux-cal: only a complex per-input timing/beamforming gain applied (`baseband *= conj(gain)`); arbitrary units; no Jy/SEFD/primary-beam. - DSA transit geometry: at meridian transit …

> AGENT

Acronyms: BIC — Bayesian Information Criterion; lnZ — log marginal likelihood (Bayesian evidence); lnB — log Bayes factor. Not BIC — the actual **Bayesian evidence ratio** (log Bayes factor) from dynesty nested sampling. Let me ground that in the numbers + confirm the evidence uncertainties, since they decide the marginal cases:

> AGENT

zsh mangled the heredoc through the double-ssh — base64-ing the Python:

> AGENT

Bayesian evidence ratios, not BIC. Precisely: **Metric:** each fit's `log_evidence` is the marginal likelihood lnZ = ln∫L(θ)π(θ)dθ, computed by **dynesty nested sampling** (it integrates likelihood over the prior; `run_joint_fit.py:174`). Component support = the **log Bayes factor** lnB = lnZ(N+1) − lnZ(N), judged on the **Kass & Raftery (1995)** scale (≈Jeffreys): 2lnB <2 bare / 2–6 positive / 6–10 strong / **>10 very strong**. dynesty also returns the evidence uncertainty (~0.5 nat/fit), so every dlnZ carries a real error bar: | burst | rung | dlnZ ± err | Kass–Raftery | |---|---|---|---| | zach | C2D1 | +3618 ± 0.8 | overwhelming | | isha | C2D1 | +536 ± 0.6 | very strong | | johndoeII | C2D1 | +208 | very strong | | johndoeII | C2D2 (2nd DSA) | **+6.7 ± 0.7** | strong (modest) | | oran | C2D1 | +179 | very strong | | mahi | C2D1 | **−291** | **disfavored** → single | | whitney | C2D1 | **+1.5 ± 0.7** | weak (2lnB≈3, boundary) | **Why evidence, not BIC:** BIC (which this repo *does* use — but only for the single-telescope emcee fits, `burstfit_modelselect.fit_models_bic`, where you have a posterior but no Z) is the *asymptotic* approximation −2lnZ ≈ −2lnL_max + k·ln(n). The joint nested fits give Z **directly**, and the Occam penalty is the exact Bayesian one: the gain-marginal kernel integrates the per-channel gains under a proper N(0,s²) prior → a `−½ ln det(I + (s²/σ²)M)` term that grows with component count (`burstfit_joint.py`). So an unhelpful component is penalized by the marginal likelihood itself — no k·ln(n) surrogate needed. Strictly better than BIC when you can afford the evidence integral. **The caveat that makes whitney's +1.5 not yet final:** s² is ML-*profiled* per rung → these are empirical-Bayes (profile-Z), not clean marginals. Irrelevant …
